8. **Composed-claims discipline + mechanical source-class reduction lattice** — *(Wave 17 fire-31 v1 codification per codex T1 fire-28 conf=0.87; UPGRADED at fire-36 per codex T1 fire-33 NEEDS-REVISION on T2 b8fa7dd1 finding "weakest-source rule not computable" conf=0.72)* — When a single normative claim cites MULTIPLE source classes, the cite MUST declare:

   - `effective_tier=<class>` — the EFFECTIVE class of the composed claim, computed by the reduction lattice below

   **Source-class precedence/order (lower wins; weakest dominates)**:
   1. `UNKNOWN` (no cite at all) — always disqualifying; reject claim until cited

   **Source-class precedence/order (lower wins; weakest dominates)**:
   1. `UNKNOWN` (no cite at all) — always disqualifying; reject claim until cited
   2. `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` (any local glue / sss-novel composition over TIER-1/TIER-2 substrates)
   3. `TIER-3-LOCAL-OPERATOR-DERIVED` (sss dogfood, codex verdicts, incidents, operator decisions)
   4. `TIER-3-LOCAL-CONFIG` (sss live config or script wiring)
   5. `TIER-2` (user-curated/sibling docs, NOT upstream authority)

   4. `TIER-3-LOCAL-CONFIG` (sss live config or script wiring)
   5. `TIER-2` (user-curated/sibling docs, NOT upstream authority)
   6. `TIER-1-COMPOSED` (ALL constituents TIER-1 with disclosed local glue) — still TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION as effective tier
   7. `TIER-1-DIRECT` (upstream verbatim file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL with anchor)

   **Reduction rule (mechanical)**: `effective_tier = MIN_PRECEDENCE(constituents)`. Concretely: if ANY constituent contains "TIER-3-LOCAL-*" OR the composition glue itself is local-codified (e.g., sss-novel composition pattern), the effective tier is `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` regardless of how many TIER-1 primitives are cited. EXCEPTION: if the composed claim is ONLY about an upstream primitive's EXISTENCE (e.g., "asyncRewake field exists" cited verbatim from Anthropic docs), it remains `TIER-1-DIRECT` because there's no local composition glue.

   7. `TIER-1-DIRECT` (upstream verbatim file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL with anchor)

   **Reduction rule (mechanical)**: `effective_tier = MIN_PRECEDENCE(constituents)`. Concretely: if ANY constituent contains "TIER-3-LOCAL-*" OR the composition glue itself is local-codified (e.g., sss-novel composition pattern), the effective tier is `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` regardless of how many TIER-1 primitives are cited. EXCEPTION: if the composed claim is ONLY about an upstream primitive's EXISTENCE (e.g., "asyncRewake field exists" cited verbatim from Anthropic docs), it remains `TIER-1-DIRECT` because there's no local composition glue.

   **Anti-promotion rule**: composition CANNOT promote a TIER-2/TIER-3 component into TIER-1. The cite class of the COMPOSED claim is determined by the MIN_PRECEDENCE constituent OR by the composition-glue class, whichever is weaker.

Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:3:description: End-to-end onboarding for a freshly-plugged-in M5Stack ESP32 device (Cardputer, Cardputer-Adv, Core, CoreS3, Stick) — detect on USB, flash UIFlow 2.0 firmware, and install the Claude Buddy MicroPython app bundle. Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:8:This skill automates the full cold-start workflow for an M5Stack ESP32 device: detect on USB, identify model, flash UIFlow 2.0, and push a MicroPython app bundle onto `/flash/` so the device boots into user software. The apps we ship (Claude Buddy, Snake, Hello) talk over BLE or USB. The workflow runs on macOS, Linux, and Windows; the skill was developed against an M5Stack Basic v2.6 (CH9102 bridge, ESP32-D0WDQ6-V3, 16 MB flash) and generalized to cover the rest of the Core family, with the Cardputer-Adv (ESP32-S3, native USB) as the current default target.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:12:This skill ships as part of the `cwc-makers` plugin for reference, but the executable scripts and the `buddy/` app bundle live in a local clone of https://github.com/moremas/build-with-claude (the `/maker-setup` command creates this clone). Run every `scripts/*.py` invocation below from inside that clone's `onboard/` directory so `--apps buddy` resolves to the sibling `buddy/device/` payload.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:28:The rig this skill lives on provisions **Cardputer-Adv** boards overwhelmingly, so `onboard.py` now defaults to `--variant cardputer-adv`. In practice that means:
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:30:- If the user says nothing about the model, go with the default. They're almost certainly holding a Cardputer-Adv.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:31:- If the user says "Cardputer" (no "Adv"), ask — the two models share a form factor but take different firmware images, and flashing the wrong one boot-loops the device.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:33:- The chip is ESP32-S3 either way, and `detect.py` won't be able to tell Cardputer from Cardputer-Adv before UIFlow is flashed (same native USB-JTAG VID, no pre-flash I2C probe). So this is a user-intent question, not a hardware-fingerprint one.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:39:The default provisioning command (fresh Cardputer-Adv, install the buddy bundle):

Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:58:The flash stage **cannot proceed without a manual button press** on native-USB boards — there is no software path. When the monitored log shows `Enter download mode` (or the script appears to wait at the FLASH stage), you MUST stop and tell the user to do the following on the **back of the Cardputer**, in your own words, before continuing:
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:84:2. **Identify** (`detect.py`) — alongside port discovery, `detect.py` reads the factory-test partition signature and/or scans I2C once UIFlow is on, and cross-references `references/hardware_signatures.md` to suggest the right firmware variant (Basic-16MB, Core2, CoreS3, Cardputer-Adv, etc.). User-facing variant choice happens via `onboard.py --variant`; there is no separate `detect.py --identify` flag.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:94:- **Native-USB ESP32-S3 boards (Cardputer, Cardputer-Adv, CoreS3) require a physical BtnG0+BtnRST dance to enter download mode.** There is no software path. The chip has no DTR/RTS bridge, so nothing esptool or pyserial can do will put it into the ROM bootloader — the user has to hold GPIO0 low across a reset pulse with the hardware buttons. On Cardputer-Adv specifically both buttons (BtnG0 and BtnRST) are on the **back of the device** — small, flush-mounted, often easiest to press with a fingernail. `onboard.py:_wait_for_download_port` prompts for this at runtime during FLASH: *press and HOLD BtnG0, briefly press BtnRST, release BtnRST first, keep holding BtnG0 for ~1 more second, release BtnG0, screen should be fully dark.* If the device reboots back into UIFlow instead, BtnG0 was released too early — the coaching retries and tells the user to hold it longer. Do NOT try to automate this with `esptool --before default_reset` or pyserial's DTR/RTS; both are no-ops on native USB (the pins aren't wired to EN), and adding them just hides the real prompt.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:95:- **Do not unplug the device during FLASH.** Especially on native USB. A mid-flash disconnect leaves the internal flash in an inconsistent state. Mask ROM is usually reachable afterwards (press BtnG0 alone on the back, or do the full BtnG0+BtnRST dance), so the recovery is just to re-run `m5-onboard go` — it's idempotent and will re-enter download mode, re-flash, re-push apps. Don't panic and don't start opening the case; the mask ROM is in silicon and survives a corrupted flash as long as the USB PHY is intact.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:101:- **Cardputer-Adv (ESP32-S3) BLE peripherals require NVS `boot_option=2` + a custom `main.py`.** UIFlow's default `boot_option=1` starts a background Flow-pairing BLE advertise that wedges the NimBLE controller — subsequent `gap_advertise(adv_data=...)` calls from user code hit OSError(-519) "Memory Capacity Exceeded" regardless of payload shape, and the device ends up advertising with empty AD fields that iOS and the desktop Claude Buddy app filter out. The bundle's `main.py` lives at `/flash/` and takes over the boot flow (showing a simple menu over `/flash/apps/`), never touches BLE itself, and leaves the controller pristine for whichever app the user picks. `install_apps.py` now sets `boot_option=2` automatically when the bundle ships a root `main.py` — don't regress that behavior.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:105:Once `m5-onboard go` finishes at the `DONE` banner, the device is ready to use on its own:
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:107:- **Power.** Slide the switch on the right edge of the Cardputer-Adv to turn it on. Same switch turns it off. The board runs off its internal LiPo when unplugged; USB-C charges it.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:110:- **Event WiFi auto-connect.** The bundle's `main.py` connects to a hard-coded event WiFi (SSID `cardputer`) on every boot and shows the result on the LCD before the launcher menu appears. Credentials live in `buddy/device/wifi_event.py`; the connect is best-effort and the launcher always continues even if the connect fails. If you're using this bundle outside the event, edit `wifi_event.py` or remove the `_connect_wifi_with_splash()` call from `main.py`.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:133:Python itself has to exist before this skill can do anything — you can't bootstrap an interpreter from inside one. `git` is **not** required — the `/maker-setup` command falls back to downloading the GitHub tarball with `curl`+`tar` (both pre-installed on macOS, Linux, and Windows 10+) when `git --version` fails. Claude's responsible for detecting Python and installing it if missing *before* running any `scripts/*.py` invocation. Detection is just running `python3 --version` / `python --version` — if it fails, Claude fetches Python with the host's native package manager before anything else.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:157:The CH9102 USB-UART driver is still a manual install on Windows — WCH doesn't publish a winget manifest. Only needed for UART-bridge boards (Basic, Fire, Core2, StickC). Native-USB ESP32-S3 boards (Cardputer, Cardputer-Adv, CoreS3) enumerate as composite USB-CDC devices using Windows' in-box drivers and need no extra install.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:180:  2. The `buddy/device/` directory inside this repo, found via `os.path.realpath(__file__)` walking up from `install_apps.py`. Works for any clone location, including symlinked skill installs at `~/.claude/skills/m5-onboard/`.
Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/cache/claude-plugins-official/cwc-makers/1.0.0\skills\m5-onboard\SKILL.md:185:- **Firmware cache.** Downloaded firmware lands at `~/.cache/m5-onboard/` (or `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/m5-onboard/`), created at mode 0700 if missing. Cache files are MD5-verified at write time and re-verified on hit. Clearing the cache is safe; the next run re-downloads.
